import { Router } from "express";
import { config } from "../config";
import { pool } from "../db";
import { requireRole } from "../middleware/auth";
import { createOrder, isValidRazorpaySignature, paymentInfo } from "../payments";
import { generateInvoices, INVOICE_SELECT, toInvoice } from "../rental";
import { parseId } from "../spaces";

// /owner/rental — a space owner's rental invoices and paying them.
// Owners only ever reach invoices for their own spaces; anything else is 404.
export const rentalRouter = Router();

rentalRouter.use(requireRole("owner"));

async function findInvoice(id: number | null, ownerId: number) {
  if (!id) return null;
  const { rows } = await pool.query(`${INVOICE_SELECT} WHERE i.id = $1 AND s.owner_id = $2`, [id, ownerId]);
  return rows[0] ?? null;
}

// GET /owner/rental — every invoice for the owner's spaces, newest month first.
rentalRouter.get("/", async (req, res) => {
  await generateInvoices();
  const { rows } = await pool.query(
    `${INVOICE_SELECT} WHERE s.owner_id = $1 ORDER BY i.month DESC, s.name, i.id`,
    [req.user!.id]
  );
  const unpaid = rows.filter((row) => row.status === "due");
  res.json({
    invoices: rows.map(toInvoice),
    summary: {
      due_count: unpaid.length,
      overdue_count: unpaid.filter((row) => row.overdue).length,
      due_amount: Math.round(unpaid.reduce((sum, row) => sum + row.amount, 0) * 100) / 100,
    },
  });
});

// GET /owner/rental/invoices/:id — one invoice (also its receipt once paid).
rentalRouter.get("/invoices/:id", async (req, res) => {
  const invoice = await findInvoice(parseId(req.params.id), req.user!.id);
  if (!invoice) return res.status(404).json({ error: "Invoice not found" });
  res.json({ invoice: toInvoice(invoice), payment: paymentInfo(null) });
});

import type { Metadata } from "next";
import Link from "next/link";
import { invoiceStatus, RentalStatusBadge } from "@/components/RentalStatusBadge";
import { formatDate, formatMonth, formatRupees } from "@/lib/format";
import { apiGet, requireRole } from "@/lib/session";
import type { RentalInvoice } from "@/lib/types";

export const metadata: Metadata = { title: "Rental · ParkSpotZ" };

interface OwnerRental {
  invoices: RentalInvoice[];
  summary: { due_count: number; overdue_count: number; due_amount: number };
}

// What the owner pays ParkSpotZ each month for hosting their spaces.
export default async function OwnerRentalPage() {
  await requireRole("owner");
  const data = await apiGet<OwnerRental>("/owner/rental");
  const invoices = data?.invoices ?? [];

  return (
    <main className="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-3xl flex-col gap-4 px-4 py-6">
      <div>
        <h1 className="text-2xl font-bold">Rental</h1>
        <p className="mt-1 text-slate-600">
          The monthly fee for listing each of your spaces on ParkSpotZ. It depends on the number of slots. The
          month you add a space is free.
        </p>
      </div>

      {!data && <p className="text-red-700">Could not load your invoices. Please refresh the page.</p>}

      {data && data.summary.due_count > 0 && (
        <p
          className={`rounded-lg px-3 py-2 text-base font-medium ${
            data.summary.overdue_count > 0 ? "bg-red-50 text-red-800" : "bg-amber-50 text-amber-900"
          }`}
        >
          {formatRupees(data.summary.due_amount)} to pay across {data.summary.due_count}{" "}
          {data.summary.due_count === 1 ? "invoice" : "invoices"}
          {data.summary.overdue_count > 0 && ` · ${data.summary.overdue_count} overdue`}
        </p>
      )}
      {data && invoices.length === 0 && <p className="text-slate-600">No invoices yet.</p>}

      <ul className="flex flex-col gap-3">
        {invoices.map((invoice) => (
          <li
            key={invoice.id}
            className={`flex flex-col gap-2 rounded-xl border p-4 ${
              invoice.overdue ? "border-red-400 bg-red-50" : "border-slate-200 bg-white"
            }`}
          >
            <div className="flex flex-wrap items-start justify-between gap-2">
              <h2 className="min-w-0 text-lg font-semibold break-words">{invoice.space_name}</h2>
              <RentalStatusBadge status={invoiceStatus(invoice)} />
            </div>
            <p className="text-sm text-slate-700">
              {formatMonth(invoice.month)} · {invoice.tier_name} tier ({invoice.slots} slots)
            </p>
            <p className="text-sm text-slate-700">
              <span className="font-semibold text-slate-900">{formatRupees(invoice.amount)}</span> ·{" "}
              {invoice.paid_at ? `paid ${formatDate(invoice.paid_at)}` : `due ${formatDate(invoice.due_date)}`} ·{" "}
              {invoice.reference}
            </p>
            <Link
              href={`/owner/rental/${invoice.id}`}
              className={
                invoice.status === "due"
                  ? "inline-flex h-11 items-center self-start rounded-lg bg-emerald-600 px-5 text-base font-semibold text-white hover:bg-emerald-700"
                  : "inline-flex h-11 items-center self-start rounded-lg text-base font-semibold text-emerald-700 underline"
              }
            >
              {invoice.status === "due" ? "Pay" : "View receipt"}
            </Link>
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>
    </main>
  );
}

export async function loadTiers(): Promise<Tier[]> {
  const { rows } = await pool.query(
    "SELECT name, min_slots, max_slots, monthly_fee::float AS monthly_fee FROM rental_tiers ORDER BY min_slots"
  );
  return rows;
}

## Known limitations

- **Google Maps is untested.** No API key was available while building, so the map, Places search and draggable pin have not been run with a real key.
- **Razorpay is untested.** No Razorpay keys were available, so only the mock payment mode has been run end to end.
- **Mock payment trusts the browser.** In `mock` mode anyone logged in as a customer can mark their own booking paid. Never use `mock` mode on a public deployment.
- **Bookings must start and end on the same IST day**, within the space's opening hours. Overnight bookings are not supported.
- **No background job for expiry.** An unpaid booking simply stops counting once it is older than the hold time; it stays in the database as an expired booking.
- **No payment webhook.** A Razorpay payment is confirmed only when the customer's browser returns from checkout.
- **No cancellations or refunds.**
- **Entry photos live on local disk** (`backend/uploads/`). A cloud deployment will need object storage instead, because most hosts do not keep local files between restarts.
- **Number plates and vehicle photos are personal data.** There is no retention policy yet: nothing deletes old visits or photos. Decide how long to keep them before using this with real customers.
- **Overstay is collected at the gate only** (cash or UPI, recorded by the attendant). The app does not take overstay payments online and does not check that the money was actually received.
- **A removed attendant's username stays taken**, because the account is deactivated and not deleted.
- **Guest phone numbers are not verified.** There is no OTP, so a guest can type any number.
- **A lost guest receipt link cannot be recovered** except from the browser that made the booking (My bookings), and only while that browser keeps its cookie (30 days). There is no way to look a guest booking up by phone number.
- **Guest names and phone numbers are personal data** with no retention policy yet, like number plates and photos.
- **The per-IP guest limit depends on the proxy in front of the app.** The API takes the caller's address from the last `X-Forwarded-For` entry (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS=1`). Run directly as described here, with nothing in front of Next.js, a caller can forge that header and so get around the per-IP limit; the per-browser limit can be avoided by clearing cookies. When deployed, put the app behind a proxy or platform that overwrites `X-Forwarded-For` and set `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` to the number of proxies between the internet and the API.
- **No password reset and no login rate limiting.**
- **Rental has no auto-debit and no reminders.** Owners pay each invoice themselves; nobody is emailed or messaged.
- **Rental non-payment is flag-only.** An overdue invoice changes nothing about how the space works.
- **The admin cannot mark an invoice paid, cancel it or change its amount.**
- **No hosting-cost view yet.** Comparing rental income with what the platform costs to run is left for when it is deployed.
- **Spaces that are not listed are not invoiced**, so unlisting a space before the month starts avoids that month's fee.
- **Not deployed.** Everything runs locally; a phone can only use it if it can reach your computer.

Claude11 Oct 2026, 14:04

Deployment is underway, but it can't go live until you've done three sign-in steps that only you can do. In the meantime I've started preparing the app for production in the background.

The plan, from your answers

PartWhere it runs
WebsiteVercel (your account vasulearnmail-5577 is already connected)
APIGoogle Cloud Run
DatabaseNeon, a free hosted Postgres
Entry photosA Google Cloud Storage bucket
PaymentsRazorpay test keys, so no real money moves
Starting dataThe ten demo spaces and demo logins

What I need you to do

1. Find your Google Cloud project ID

You almost certainly have a usable project already: Google only issues a Maps key to a project with billing attached. Open console.cloud.google.com, click the project name in the top bar, and note the ID (not the name) of the project that holds your Maps key. Tell me that ID; it isn't a secret.

2. Install the Google Cloud command-line tool and sign in

Run these in your own Terminal app, since they're interactive and open a browser for sign-in:

curl https://sdk.cloud.google.com | bash

Then close and reopen the terminal and run:

gcloud auth login

3. Create the Neon database

Go to neon.tech, sign up, and create a project named parkspotz, choosing the region closest to India (Singapore or Mumbai if offered). Copy the connection string it shows and save it in a new file at backend/.env.production, as a single line:

DATABASE_URL=<paste the connection string here>

Don't paste the connection string into this chat; it contains the database password and the chat is public.

Things to know before going public

  • Demo passwords will be changed for the public site. parkspotz-demo is published in your GitHub repo, so anyone could log in as the demo owner. The public site will use a private password that I'll store in a local file for you.
  • Vercel's free plan is for non-commercial use. It's fine for a pilot or demo. Once the app takes real money, Vercel's terms require the Pro plan, about US$20 a month.
  • Costs with this setup should be near zero at pilot traffic: Neon's free tier, Cloud Run billed only while handling requests, and small storage charges. I'll confirm against current pricing when we set up the cost view.
  • The Maps key should be restricted to the new site address once we know it, so nobody else can use it on your bill.

Claude11 Oct 2026, 14:22

The install failed because of a permissions problem on your Mac, not anything wrong with Google's installer. There's also a side effect to clean up: two stray copies of the tool ended up inside the project folder.

Why it failed

The installer needs to create a settings folder at ~/.config/gcloud, but your ~/.config folder is owned by the system administrator account (root), not by you (vasumac). So the installer was refused:

PermissionError: [Errno 13] Permission denied: '/Users/vasumac/.config/gcloud'

It stopped before adding gcloud to your terminal, which is why gcloud auth login then said "command not found". The ~/.config folder has been that way since January, most likely from some other program installed with sudo.

The side effect

At the installer's "installation directory" question, the text cd /Users/... and ls got typed in as folder names. So the tool was unpacked three times, 311 MB each:

  • ~/google-cloud-sdk: the right place, complete and usable.
  • A folder named cd inside the project.
  • A folder named ls inside the project.

The two inside the project aren't in git, but they should go so they don't get committed by accident.

Fix

Run these in your own Terminal app, one at a time.

1. Take ownership of the settings folder. This asks for your Mac login password:

sudo chown -R vasumac ~/.config

2. Remove the two stray copies from the project:

rm -rf "/Users/vasumac/Documents/vasu_iisc/parking-app/cd " "/Users/vasumac/Documents/vasu_iisc/parking-app/ls"

3. Finish the install from the good copy. Press Enter to accept the default at each question:

~/google-cloud-sdk/install.sh

4. Close the terminal, open a new one, then sign in and set the project:

gcloud auth login
gcloud config set project project-9ccc00ad-5852-42cc-b1f

I've left steps 1 and 2 to you: the first needs your password, and the second permanently deletes folders.

After that, the remaining step is the Neon database file (backend/.env.production). Tell me when both are done.

Claude11 Oct 2026, 14:27

The install succeeded this time. gcloud isn't found only because that terminal window was opened before the install, so it hasn't picked up the change yet.

What happened

The installer finished and added gcloud to your terminal's startup file (~/.zshrc). It then printed:

==> Start a new shell for the changes to take effect.

A terminal only reads that startup file when it opens. The window you ran gcloud auth login in was already open, so it still doesn't know where gcloud is.

Fix

Either close that terminal and open a new one, or reload the settings in the current one:

source ~/.zshrc

Then sign in and set the project:

gcloud auth login
gcloud config set project project-9ccc00ad-5852-42cc-b1f

Checked

  • The tool itself runs: Google Cloud SDK version 588.0.0 from ~/google-cloud-sdk.
  • ~/.config now belongs to you, so the permissions problem is gone.
  • The two stray cd and ls folders are removed from the project.
  • You aren't signed in to Google Cloud yet; gcloud auth login will do that.

After signing in, the remaining step is the Neon database file at backend/.env.production.

You11 Oct 2026, 14:29

why is the neon.tech geting used?

Claude11 Oct 2026, 14:29

Neon is where the app's database will live once it's public. It's there because, when I asked where the production database should run, you picked "Free hosted Postgres" over Google Cloud SQL, and Neon is the free provider I suggested for that.

Why a hosted database is needed at all

The app stores everything in a PostgreSQL database: accounts, parking spaces, bookings, payments, vehicle history and rental invoices. Right now that database runs on your Mac, which works for local testing but can't serve a public website. Once the site is on Vercel and the API on Google Cloud, the database has to be somewhere they can reach around the clock.

Why Neon and not Google Cloud

Neon (your choice)Google Cloud SQL
Monthly costFree tierRoughly US$10–15, even with no traffic
Where it runsNeon's serversSame Google Cloud project as the API
Accounts to manageOne more (Neon)None extra
Behaviour when idleSleeps, so the first request after a quiet spell takes a few secondsAlways on

The database is the one part of the setup that costs money all the time on Google Cloud, which is why I offered a free alternative for a pilot.

What it means for you

  • One extra sign-up, at neon.tech.
  • Your data sits with a third company in addition to Google and Vercel.
  • The free tier has limits on storage and usage that are fine for a pilot but would need a paid plan as usage grows.

Moving to Cloud SQL later is straightforward: copy the data across and change one setting.

If you'd rather keep everything on Google Cloud and accept the monthly cost, say so and I'll set up Cloud SQL instead; then you can skip Neon entirely.

I couldn't find a Mumbai-specific monthly price for db-f1-micro. The search results give a US-region price and an official table with no region label, so the Mumbai figure below is an estimate.

**Base compute rate:** Google's official pricing page lists db-f1-micro at $0.0105 per hour, with no region named in the snippet. A third-party pricing guide lists the same $0.0105/hour rate for us-central1 at roughly $8 per month. Multiplying by 730 hours gives about $7.67 per month. Mumbai pricing may differ from us-central1, so check the region dropdown on Google's pricing page or the Pricing Calculator.

**Mumbai extended support:** Mumbai appears on Google's list of regions that carry extended-support pricing for db-f1-micro. That list shows $0.018 per hour for years 1 and 2, and $0.035 per hour for year 3. At 730 hours, that works out to roughly $13.14 per month in years 1–2 and about $25.55 per month in year 3. This source is about 620 days old, so verify these rates before relying on them. The same page lists the HA variant at double these rates.

**Other costs to account for:**
- Shared-core tiers are Enterprise-edition only. The instance price covers compute only; storage, high availability, and network egress are billed separately.
- Shared-core instances are not covered by the Cloud SQL SLA.
- A new Google Cloud account may receive credits for trying the service. New customers also get $300 in free credits to spend on Cloud SQL to run, test, and deploy workloads.

**Bottom line:** Expect about $8/month for the base instance in Mumbai if its rate matches the standard US rate, but the search did not confirm this. If Mumbai is on extended support when you deploy, the cost would be closer to $13/month. Storage, backups, and egress are extra. For an exact figure, use the Google Cloud Pricing Calculator with asia-south1, PostgreSQL, Enterprise edition, and db-f1-micro.

**Cloud Run free tier (monthly)**

The official Cloud Run pricing page says the free tier is based on us-central1 pricing, and Cloud Run pricing depends on the selected region. The allowances differ by billing mode:

- **Request-based billing:** the page lists 180,000 vCPU-seconds, 360,000 GiB-seconds of memory, and 2 million requests per month. Third-party guides agree on these figures, e.g. First 180,000 vCPU-seconds per month are free. and First 2 million requests per month are free.
- **Instance-based billing:** the page lists 240,000 vCPU-seconds free and 450,000 GiB-seconds of RAM per month. No separate free request allowance applies in this mode, per First 240,000 vCPU-seconds and 450,000 GiB-seconds per month are free.. and No free requests allowance applies in this mode.

The free tier is aggregated across projects by billing account and resets every month.

**Cloud Storage Standard price in asia-south1 (Mumbai)**

I could not find a Mumbai-specific Standard storage rate. The figures available are all US or multi-region:

- Regional US Standard storage is commonly cited at $0.020/GB-month, e.g. Standard | $0.020/GB-month | None | None | None in a 2026 guide.
- US multi-region Standard is higher, at Multi-region Standard costs $0.026/GB versus $0.020/GB for single-region.
- A 2026 pricing article says Region (us-central1 baseline, with up to 15% premium for Asia regions). This is a general third-party claim, not a confirmed asia-south1 price, so treat it as an estimate.

The only Mumbai pricing I found was for Persistent Disk, not Cloud Storage, so I can't give a verified asia-south1 figure. To confirm it, check the Cloud Storage pricing page on cloud.google.com with the region set to asia-south1, or use the Google Cloud Pricing Calculator.

Claude11 Oct 2026, 14:59

For this app at pilot traffic, Google Cloud costs roughly US$0–1 a month with the Neon database, or about US$10–15 a month if the database also runs on Google Cloud. The database is nearly the whole difference.

Cost by part

PartWhat it doesExpected monthly cost
Cloud RunRuns the APIUS$0 at pilot traffic. The free allowance is 2 million requests and 180,000 vCPU-seconds a month, and you pay nothing while idle.
Cloud StorageStores entry photosA few cents. Around US$0.02 per GB a month, and each photo is under 1 MB, so 1,000 photos is well under a gigabyte.
Build and image storageBuilds the API container on each deployUsually a few cents, often free.
Cloud SQL (only if you skip Neon)The databaseAbout US$8–13 for the smallest instance, plus storage and backups, so US$10–15 in practice. It's charged every hour whether or not anyone uses the app.

Outside Google Cloud's hosting bill

  • Google Maps: billed on the same Google account, per map load and per place search. Each service has a free monthly allowance, which a pilot is unlikely to exceed.
  • Vercel: free on the Hobby plan for non-commercial use; about US$20 a month on Pro once the app takes real money.
  • Razorpay: no monthly fee; a percentage of each real payment, nothing in test mode.
  • Neon: free tier.

Caveats on these numbers

  • I couldn't confirm Mumbai-specific prices. The Cloud SQL figure of about US$8 is the US rate for the smallest instance; one source suggests Mumbai may be closer to US$13. Storage in Asian regions can run up to about 15% above US rates.
  • New Google Cloud accounts usually get US$300 in free credit, which would cover Cloud SQL for many months if your account still has it.
  • Costs scale with use. Heavy traffic would move Cloud Run and Maps out of their free allowances.

For exact figures, Google's pricing calculator lets you pick Mumbai (asia-south1) and the specific services.

I'd also set a budget alert in Google Cloud at around US$10 when we deploy, so you get an email if spending goes above what's expected.

Do you want to stay with Neon, or move the database to Cloud SQL?

  • Cloud SQL pricing
  • Google Cloud SQL Pricing — Every Machine Type (Bytebase)

  • Google Cloud Storage Pricing (CloudZero)

## 4. API (Cloud Run)

### 4.1 Secrets

Store the secret values in Secret Manager, not as plain environment variables. 👤 You type or paste the values yourself:

```bash
gcloud services enable run.googleapis.com artifactregistry.googleapis.com secretmanager.googleapis.com

for name in parkspotz-database-url parkspotz-jwt-secret parkspotz-razorpay-key-secret; do
  printf "Value for %s: " "$name"; read -s value; echo
  printf "%s" "$value" | gcloud secrets create "$name" --data-file=-
  gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding "$name" \
    --member="serviceAccount:parkspotz-api@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com" \
    --role="roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor"
done
unset value
```

Use the **pooled** Neon connection string for `parkspotz-database-url`.

### 4.2 Build and push the image

Cloud Run needs a `linux/amd64` image (a Mac with Apple silicon builds `arm64` unless told otherwise):

```bash
gcloud artifacts repositories create parkspotz --repository-format=docker --location=asia-south1
gcloud auth configure-docker asia-south1-docker.pkg.dev

IMAGE=asia-south1-docker.pkg.dev/<PROJECT_ID>/parkspotz/api:$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)
docker build --platform linux/amd64 -t "$IMAGE" backend
docker push "$IMAGE"
```

The image contains the compiled API and its production dependencies. It has no `.env` file, no tests and no uploads, and it runs as a non-root user.

### 4.3 Deploy

The first deployment needs the website's address for `APP_BASE_URL`, which you do not have yet. Deploy with a placeholder such as `https://example.invalid`, then set the real one in step 6.

```bash
gcloud run deploy parkspotz-api \
  --image "$IMAGE" \
  --region asia-south1 \
  --allow-unauthenticated \
  --service-account parkspotz-api@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com \
  --execution-environment gen2 \
  --add-volume name=uploads,type=cloud-storage,bucket=<BUCKET_NAME> \
  --add-volume-mount volume=uploads,mount-path=/mnt/uploads \
  --set-secrets DATABASE_URL=parkspotz-database-url:latest,JWT_SECRET=parkspotz-jwt-secret:latest,RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET=parkspotz-razorpay-key-secret:latest \
  --set-env-vars PAYMENT_MODE=razorpay,RAZORPAY_KEY_ID=<RAZORPAY_TEST_KEY_ID>,APP_BASE_URL=https://example.invalid,UPLOADS_DIR=/mnt/uploads,TRUST_PROXY_HOPS=2,APP_VERSION=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD) \
  --min-instances 0 --max-instances 2 --memory 512Mi
```

`--allow-unauthenticated` is needed because Vercel calls the API over the public internet; the API does its own login checks. Note the service URL that the command prints (`https://parkspotz-api-xxxx.a.run.app`).

Every setting the API reads:

| Name | Purpose | Secret? | Example / placeholder |
|------|---------|---------|-----------------------|
| `DATABASE_URL` | Postgres connection string (Neon pooled endpoint) | **Yes** | `postgres://<user>:<password>@<host>-pooler.<region>.aws.neon.tech/<db>?sslmode=require` |
| `JWT_SECRET` | Signs login and guest cookies; at least 32 characters | **Yes** | 96 random hex characters |
| `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` | Razorpay test key secret | **Yes** | from the Razorpay dashboard |
| `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` | Razorpay test key id (sent to browsers by design) | No | `rzp_test_...` |
| `PAYMENT_MODE` | Must be `razorpay`. The API refuses to start in production with `mock` | No | `razorpay` |
| `APP_BASE_URL` | Public `https://` address of the website; receipt QR codes link to it | No | `https://<your-site>.vercel.app` |
| `UPLOADS_DIR` | Folder for entry photos; the bucket's mount path | No | `/mnt/uploads` |
| `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` | Proxies in front of the API whose client address is trusted (section 7) | No | `2` |
| `PG_POOL_MAX` | Database connections kept open; default 5 | No | `5` |
| `APP_VERSION` | Label shown by the diagnostics page | No | a git commit |
| `BOOKING_HOLD_MINUTES` | Unpaid booking hold; default 10 | No | `10` |
| `ENTRY_GRACE_MINUTES` | Entry allowed this long before a booking starts; default 15 | No | `15` |
| `OVERSTAY_GRACE_MINUTES`, `OVERSTAY_BLOCK_MINUTES`, `OVERSTAY_RATE_PERCENT` | Overstay rule; defaults 10, 30, 100 | No | |
| `GUEST_MAX_PENDING` | Unpaid guest holds per browser, address and phone; default 2 | No | `2` |
| `RENTAL_DUE_DAY` | Day of the month rental falls due; default 7 | No | `7` |
| `NODE_ENV` | Set to `production` by the image. Do not override | No | |
| `PORT` | Set by Cloud Run. Do not set | No | |

`ADMIN_USERNAME`, `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `DEMO_PASSWORD`, `SEED_DEMO_DATA` and `CONFIRM_REMOTE` are used only by the scripts in section 2. Do not give them to the Cloud Run service.

With `NODE_ENV=production` the API will not start if `PAYMENT_MODE` resolves to `mock`, if `JWT_SECRET` is shorter than 32 characters, or if `APP_BASE_URL` is not an `https://` address. If a deployment fails to become ready, read the service's logs; the reason is printed there.

Check it is up: open `https://<CLOUD_RUN_URL>/health`. It should show `{"status":"ok","db":"ok"}`.

---

## 5. Website (Vercel)

1. 👤 In Vercel, import the GitHub repository as a new project.
2. Set **Root Directory** to `frontend`. Framework: Next.js (detected). Leave the build and output settings alone.
3. Add these environment variables (Production, and Preview if you use previews) **before** the first build:

   | Name | Purpose | Secret? | Value |
   |------|---------|---------|-------|
   | `API_URL` | Where `/api/*` is forwarded and where server-rendered pages fetch data | No | `https://<CLOUD_RUN_URL>` (no trailing slash) |
   | `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` | Google Maps key. It is visible to browsers by design; protect it with restrictions (section 6) | No, but restrict it | your key |

   These are the only two variables the website uses.
4. Deploy, and note the site address (`https://<your-site>.vercel.app` or your own domain).

Fixed at build time: the `/api/*` forwarding address (from `API_URL`) and the Maps key are baked in when the site is built. After changing either, **redeploy** the Vercel project.

Login cookies: the API sets them with `HttpOnly`, `Secure` (in production), `SameSite=Lax` and no `Domain`, and they travel through Vercel's forwarding, so the browser stores them for the Vercel site's own domain. Nothing needs configuring.

Vercel's free plan is for personal, non-commercial use; check its terms before taking real customers.

## 2. Database (Neon)

1. 👤 In Neon, create a project and a database. Neon shows two connection strings:
   - the **direct** one (host like `ep-xxxx.<region>.aws.neon.tech`), and
   - the **pooled** one (host contains `-pooler`).

   Both end in `?sslmode=require`. Use the **direct** string for the one-off scripts below and the **pooled** string for the running API.

2. From `backend/` on your computer, create the tables and seed data. Put the connection string in the command's environment; do not edit `backend/.env`. Each script prints the host and database name it is about to change, and refuses a remote database without `CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes`.

   ```bash
   cd backend
   npm install

   # so the secrets do not end up in your shell history, read them in first:
   read -s DATABASE_URL    # paste the DIRECT Neon connection string, press Enter
   read -s DEMO_PASSWORD
   read -s ADMIN_PASSWORD
   export DATABASE_URL DEMO_PASSWORD ADMIN_PASSWORD

   CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes npm run db:setup          # creates the tables; safe to repeat
   CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes npm run db:seed           # demo accounts, ten demo spaces, rental tiers
   CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes ADMIN_USERNAME=admin npm run admin:create
   CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes npm run rental:generate   # optional; pages do this on demand

   unset DATABASE_URL DEMO_PASSWORD ADMIN_PASSWORD
   ```

   Check the first line each script prints: it must name the Neon host, not `127.0.0.1`.

   To start without demo data, run the seed as `SEED_DEMO_DATA=false CONFIRM_REMOTE=yes npm run db:seed` (rental tiers only; no `DEMO_PASSWORD` needed).

Notes:

- `sslmode=require` works as is. The database driver prints a notice that it treats `require` as full certificate verification; that is fine with Neon. Writing `sslmode=verify-full` silences the notice.
- The API uses no session-level database features (no `LISTEN`, advisory locks or `SET`), and its row and table locks are always inside one transaction, so the pooled endpoint is safe for it.
- The API keeps at most `PG_POOL_MAX` connections open (default 5).
- A free Neon database goes to sleep when idle. The first request afterwards takes a few seconds while it wakes.

---

Bash: Generate private passwords for the public site into the local ignored file, then create the tables on Neon
python3 -I - <<'EOF'
import secrets, re
p='backend/.env.production'
s=open(p).read()
keys={l.split('=',1)[0].strip() for l in s.splitlines() if '=' in l and not l.strip().startswith('#')}
add=[]
if 'ADMIN_USERNAME' not in keys: add.append('ADMIN_USERNAME=admin')
if 'ADMIN_PASSWORD' not in keys: add.append('ADMIN_PASSWORD='+secrets.token_urlsafe(18))
if 'DEMO_PASSWORD' not in keys: add.append('DEMO_PASSWORD='+secrets.token_urlsafe(12))
if add:
    open(p,'a').write(('' if s.endswith('\n') else '\n')+'\n# Logins for the PUBLIC site (generated; not in git)\n'+'\n'.join(add)+'\n')
print('added:',[a.split('=')[0] for a in add])
EOF
cat > /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-vasumac-Documents-vasu-iisc-parking-app/bef924fd-6088-48d5-9cef-55dd7bdfa2f1/scratchpad/prodrun.py <<'EOF'
"""Run a backend npm script against the production database, loading values
from backend/.env.production without echoing them."""
import os, subprocess, sys
env = dict(os.environ)
vals = {}
for line in open("backend/.env.production"):
    line = line.strip()
    if line and not line.startswith("#") and "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        vals[k.strip()] = v.strip().strip('"').strip("'")
env["DATABASE_URL"] = vals["DIRECT_DATABASE_URL"]
for k in ("ADMIN_USERNAME", "ADMIN_PASSWORD", "DEMO_PASSWORD"):
    env[k] = vals[k]
env["CONFIRM_REMOTE"] = "yes"
env["PATH"] = "/Users/vasumac/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:" + env["PATH"]
r = subprocess.run(["npm", "run", "-s", sys.argv[1]], cwd="backend", env=env, capture_output=True, text=True)
out = (r.stdout + r.stderr)
for secret in (vals["DIRECT_DATABASE_URL"], vals["DATABASE_URL"], vals["ADMIN_PASSWORD"], vals["DEMO_PASSWORD"]):
    out = out.replace(secret, "***")
print(out.strip()[-1500:]); print("exit", r.returncode)
EOF

You11 Oct 2026, 15:55

how do we restrict the maps key to the site address

Claude11 Oct 2026, 15:56

Restricting the key takes about two minutes in the Google Cloud console. It tells Google to accept your Maps key only when the request comes from your site, so nobody can copy the key from the page and run up usage on your bill.

Steps

  1. Open the Credentials page for your project. Sign in with the same Google account if asked.
  2. Under API keys, click the name of your Maps key to open it.
  3. Under Application restrictions, select Websites.
  4. Under Website restrictions, click Add and enter each of these, one at a time:
https://parkspotz.vercel.app/*
http://localhost:3000/*
  1. Under API restrictions, select Restrict key, open the dropdown, and tick these two:
  2. Maps JavaScript API
  3. Places API (New)
  4. Click Save.

Changes can take up to five minutes to apply.

What each part does

  • Website restrictions limit where the key works. The first line covers the live site; the second keeps the map working when you run the app on your Mac.
  • API restrictions limit what the key can be used for. Without this, a copied key could be used for other paid Google services in your project.

Check it worked

After a few minutes, open https://parkspotz.vercel.app, search for a destination, and confirm the map and place suggestions still appear. If the map area shows an error instead, the usual cause is a typo in the website line, such as a missing /* at the end.

If you add your own domain later

Come back to the same screen and add it, for example https://www.yourdomain.com/*. Vercel's preview links for individual deployments use different addresses and won't show the map unless you also add https://*.vercel.app/*, which I'd leave out since it would allow any Vercel-hosted site to use your key.

Claude11 Oct 2026, 16:13

The walkthrough video is ready: demo/parkspotz-walkthrough.mp4, 3 minutes 23 seconds, with no sound. I haven't watched it end to end myself; it was checked by asserting each step during recording and reviewing still frames.

One thing first: there's a file called recovery-codes.txt in the project folder that could have been published by accident. I've made git ignore it; please move it somewhere safe outside the project.

About the recovery-codes file

  • It appeared in the project folder this afternoon and was never committed, so it has not been published.
  • I didn't open it. If it holds account recovery codes (for GitHub, Neon, Vercel or Google), anyone with them could get into that account.
  • The repo is public and I commit regularly, so I added it to .gitignore as protection. Moving it out of the project folder, into a password manager for example, is the proper fix.

What the video shows

StartChapterScreen
0:00A guest searches, books, pays and gets a receipt with a QR codePhone
0:41The attendant records entry with a photo, sees the timer, then an overstay and exit with cash collectedPhone
1:31A customer's bookings and a receipt with entry, exit and overstayPhone
1:49The owner adds an attendant, views vehicle history and stats, and pays a rental invoiceDesktop
2:42Your admin dashboard, the spaces table and rental invoicesDesktop

Each chapter has a caption bar. The five chapters are also saved as separate clips in the same folder.

How it was made

  • Recorded on your Mac with throwaway data, not on the live site. Your local data is unchanged.
  • Payments use the built-in test-payment screen, not Razorpay's checkout.
  • Two scenes were staged: the overstay in chapter 2 and the finished visit in chapter 3 were set up by adjusting times in the throwaway database, since nobody waited 50 real minutes. The charges shown were calculated by the app.
  • Phone chapters are a little soft when viewed full screen, because they're recorded at phone size and enlarged.
  • The video files are not on GitHub. Only the recording script and chapter notes were committed.

Rough edges noticed while recording

None of these break anything; tell me which you'd like fixed:

  1. Place search isn't focused on Bangalore. Typing "MG Road Metro Station" offers one in Kochi first.
  2. The destination box clears after you pick a place, leaving only a small "Searching near…" line.
  3. The results map is zoomed out too far on a phone, so pins overlap.
  4. Amounts have no comma separators (₹16500 instead of ₹16,500).
  5. The top bar wraps onto two lines on a phone when logged in.
  6. Owner and admin pages are narrow on a desktop screen, with a lot of empty space.

I'd fix 1, 3 and 4 first, since customers see those.

"use client";

import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { loadGoogleMaps, PIN_ICONS } from "@/lib/googleMaps";
import { BANGALORE_CENTER, type Place } from "@/lib/landmarks";
import type { SearchResult } from "@/lib/types";

// Google map for the search screen: a blue pin for the destination, a circle
// for the search radius and one pin per parking space (green when selected).
// Only rendered when a Maps key is set.
export function ResultsMap({
  destination,
  radius,
  results,
  selectedId,
  onSelect,
}: {
  destination: Place | null;
  radius: number;
  results: SearchResult[];
  selectedId: number | null;
  onSelect: (id: number) => void;
}) {
  const hostRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const mapRef = useRef<google.maps.Map | null>(null);
  const markersRef = useRef(new Map<number, google.maps.Marker>());
  const onSelectRef = useRef(onSelect);
  const [ready, setReady] = useState(false);
  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    onSelectRef.current = onSelect;
  }, [onSelect]);

  // Create the map once.
  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    loadGoogleMaps()
      .then(() => {
        if (cancelled || !hostRef.current) return;
        mapRef.current = new google.maps.Map(hostRef.current, {
          center: BANGALORE_CENTER,
          zoom: 12,
          mapTypeControl: false,
          streetViewControl: false,
          fullscreenControl: false,
        });
        setReady(true);
      })
      .catch(() => {
        if (!cancelled) setFailed(true);
      });
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      mapRef.current = null;
    };
  }, []);

  // Redraw the destination, radius and space pins whenever the search changes.
  useEffect(() => {
    const map = mapRef.current;
    if (!ready || !map || !destination) return;

    const center = { lat: destination.lat, lng: destination.lng };
    const destinationPin = new google.maps.Marker({
      map,
      position: center,
      title: destination.name,
      icon: PIN_ICONS.destination,
    });
    const circle = new google.maps.Circle({
      map,
      center,
      radius,
      strokeColor: "#059669",
      strokeOpacity: 0.6,
      strokeWeight: 1,
      fillColor: "#059669",
      fillOpacity: 0.06,
      clickable: false,
    });
    const bounds = circle.getBounds();
    if (bounds) map.fitBounds(bounds);

    const markers = markersRef.current;
    for (const space of results) {
      const marker = new google.maps.Marker({
        map,
        position: { lat: space.lat, lng: space.lng },
        title: `${space.name} — ${space.slots_available} of ${space.total_slots} slots available`,
        icon: PIN_ICONS.space,
      });
      marker.addListener("click", () => onSelectRef.current(space.id));
      markers.set(space.id, marker);
    }

    return () => {
      destinationPin.setMap(null);
      circle.setMap(null);
      markers.forEach((marker) => marker.setMap(null));
      markers.clear();
    };
  }, [ready, destination, radius, results]);

  // Highlight the selected space's pin and bring it into view.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!ready) return;
    markersRef.current.forEach((marker, id) => {
      const selected = id === selectedId;
      marker.setIcon(selected ? PIN_ICONS.selected : PIN_ICONS.space);
      marker.setZIndex(selected ? 1000 : null);
      const position = marker.getPosition();
      if (selected && position) mapRef.current?.panTo(position);
    });
  }, [ready, selectedId, results]);

  if (failed) {
    return (
      <p className="rounded-lg border border-dashed border-slate-300 bg-white px-3 py-2 text-sm text-slate-500">
        The map could not be loaded. Check the Google Maps API key. Results are listed below.
      </p>
    );
  }
  return (
    <div
      ref={hostRef}
      aria-label="Map of parking spaces"
      className="h-72 w-full overflow-hidden rounded-xl border border-slate-200 bg-slate-100 lg:h-[32rem]"
    />
  );
}

Claude11 Oct 2026, 16:37

All three are done: the Maps key restriction is confirmed, the spending alert is set, and the three fixes are live on https://parkspotz.vercel.app.

Maps key restriction: confirmed

I read the key's settings from Google Cloud and then tested the key itself from different addresses:

Request fromResult
https://parkspotz.vercel.appAllowed
http://localhost:3000Allowed
A different websiteBlocked
A different vercel.app siteBlocked
No website at allBlocked

The website restriction is exactly the two addresses I suggested. A stolen copy of the key won't work anywhere else.

One part is looser than I recommended: the key is allowed for 32 Google Maps services, not just the two the app uses (Maps JavaScript API and Places API (New)). That's Google's default "Maps" selection. The website restriction already blocks outside use, so this is a minor tightening, not a hole. I can narrow it to the two for you if you'd like.

Spending alert: created

  • Budget: ₹1,000 a month on this Google Cloud project (your billing account is in rupees; that's roughly the US$10 I suggested).
  • Emails: at 50%, 90% and 100% of that amount, sent to the billing account's administrators, which is your Google account.
  • It only warns; it doesn't stop or cap anything.

The three fixes: live

  1. Place search favours Bangalore. Typing "MG Road Metro Station" used to offer one in Kochi first; the top suggestions are now Bengaluru's MG Road.
  2. The map frames the results. It now zooms to the destination and the spaces found, so the pins are spread apart and readable. With no results it shows the whole search radius as before.
  3. Rupee amounts have separators, in the Indian style: ₹1,500, ₹16,500, ₹1,00,000.

I checked the place suggestions and the map on the local copy, and confirmed the new build is what the live site is serving. I didn't re-check the map on the live site itself.

My first attempt at the map fix left the map blank, from a zoom setting that doesn't work with this map type. I removed that setting before committing, so it never went live.

Pushed as 3f6de89. The fixes were all in the website, so the API didn't need redeploying.

Still open

  • Move recovery-codes.txt out of the project folder.
  • The other three rough edges: the destination box clearing after you pick a place, the top bar wrapping on a phone, and narrow owner and admin pages on desktop.
  • The hosting-cost view on your admin dashboard.
  • Your decisions on data retention, billing unlisted spaces, and overstay dates in stats.
You11 Oct 2026, 17:02

How can i get the link for the chat_history from the github

Claude11 Oct 2026, 17:02

The file is in your repository at this address:

https://github.com/svasudev79/parkspotz/blob/main/chat-history.html

To get there by clicking: open github.com/svasudev79/parkspotz and click chat-history.html in the file list.

The catch: GitHub shows the code, not the page

That link displays the file's HTML source, because GitHub doesn't render HTML files. To read it as a formatted page, use one of these:

Option 1: download it. On the file's page, click the download button (the arrow icon at the top right of the file view), then open the downloaded file in your browser.

Option 2: turn on GitHub Pages, which gives you a proper shareable link:

  1. In the repository, go to Settings → Pages.
  2. Under Build and deployment, set Source to "Deploy from a branch".
  3. Choose branch main and folder / (root), then Save.
  4. After a minute or two, the chat history is at:

https://svasudev79.github.io/parkspotz/chat-history.html

GitHub Pages is free for public repositories and updates by itself every time the file changes.

Things to know

  • It's public. Anyone with either link can read the whole conversation, including your email address and the terminal output. I scan it for keys and passwords before every commit, and none have been published.
  • It's always one reply behind. I refresh the file each time I commit, and the export is taken before the reply that reports it.
  • The copy on your Mac is the same file: chat-history.html in the project folder, which opens directly in a browser.

If you want the Pages link, I can't switch that setting on for you, since it's in your GitHub account settings; the four steps above take about a minute.

You11 Oct 2026, 17:19

how do i prepare for an interview on basis of this work

Claude11 Oct 2026, 17:19

Prepare to explain this as a product you specified and directed, with AI doing most of the coding, and make sure you can defend every design decision in it. Interviewers will care less about how many features there are than about whether you understand why it works the way it does.

1. Be upfront about how it was built

The chat history is public in your repository, so anyone who looks will see the app was built with Claude. Say so first, then show what was yours:

  • You wrote the problem statement and spec.
  • You made the product calls: guest booking, the attendant role, overstay rules, tiered rental, test keys before live.
  • You made the cost and hosting trade-offs: Neon over Cloud SQL, Vercel plus Cloud Run.
  • You tested it and got it deployed, including debugging real problems (the GitHub token and email privacy errors, the Google Cloud install permissions).

"I directed an AI to build this and can explain all of it" is a strong position. Claiming you hand-wrote it and then failing a follow-up question is not.

2. Have a two-minute pitch

Cover, in order: the problem (parking discovery in Bangalore is fragmented, with no pre-booking), who uses it (customers, space owners, gate attendants, you as platform admin), what it does end to end, and that it's live. Then offer a demo; you have the site and a 3-minute video.

3. Know the architecture well enough to draw it

Practise sketching this from memory and explaining each arrow:

  • Browser → Next.js site on Vercel → Express API on Cloud Run → Postgres on Neon.
  • Razorpay for payments, Google Maps for search and the map, a storage bucket for entry photos.

Be ready for "why": why the site forwards API calls instead of the browser calling the API directly (login cookies on iPhones), why a relational database, why the API scales to zero.

4. The questions most likely to come up

These are the technically interesting parts. For each, read the code until you can explain it without notes:

QuestionWhere to look
How do you stop two people booking the last slot?backend/src/routes/bookings.ts: one transaction that locks the space row, recounts, then inserts
How is availability calculated for a time window?backend/src/availability.ts: overlapping bookings, with unpaid ones expiring after 10 minutes
How do you know a payment is real?backend/src/payments.ts: the server verifies Razorpay's signature; the price is computed on the server
How do logins and roles work?backend/src/middleware/auth.ts: signed cookie, role checks on every route
How does a guest own a booking with no account?backend/src/routes/guest.ts: an unguessable token in a signed cookie and in the receipt link
How is nearby search done?Haversine distance in SQL, in backend/src/routes/spaces.ts
How is overstay charged?Grace period, then 30-minute blocks, computed on the server at exit
How did you test it?42 automated API tests, especially the last-slot race

Run the app locally and step through one booking while reading the code. That's the fastest way to make it stick.

5. Know its weaknesses before they find them

Raising limitations yourself shows judgement. The honest list:

  • Guest phone numbers aren't verified (no OTP).
  • No payment webhook, so a payment is only confirmed when the customer's browser returns.
  • No cancellations or refunds; no overnight bookings.
  • Overstay is collected in cash or UPI at the gate, unverified.
  • No password reset or login rate limiting.
  • Personal data (number plates, photos, phone numbers) has no retention policy yet.
  • It runs on test payment keys and demo data.

For each, have one sentence on how you'd fix it. README.md has the full list under "Known limitations".

6. Prepare three stories

Interviewers remember specifics:

  1. A trade-off you made: Neon versus Cloud SQL, with the cost reasoning.
  2. Something that went wrong: the staged files that vanished from the working folder at the start, or the deployment permissions failures, and what you did.
  3. A product decision: why attendants see no revenue but do see one vehicle's overstay amount.

7. Expect "what would you do next?"

Good answers: real payments with KYC, OTP for guests, a native mobile app (your Phase 2), ParkSpotZ-managed spaces, bike parking, and measuring actual usage before building more.

What I'd do this week

  1. Read the six backend files in the table above, top to bottom.
  2. Explain the last-slot logic and payment verification aloud until it's fluent.
  3. Run the automated tests and read two of them.
  4. Do the demo three times against the live site so it's smooth.

I can write this up as an interview prep document in the project, with model answers to the likely questions and a diagram of the architecture, or run a mock interview here where I ask the questions and give feedback on your answers. Which would help more?

// SQL shared by search (and, later, booking) so both count slots the same way.

// Condition on a bookings row aliased `b`: true when it still holds a slot.
// Confirmed bookings always do; pending (unpaid) ones only while younger than
// the hold duration, passed in minutes as the parameter at `holdParam`.
export function holdsSlotSql(holdParam: string): string {
  return `(b.status = 'confirmed'
    OR (b.status = 'pending' AND b.created_at > now() - make_interval(mins => ${holdParam}::int)))`;
}

// Number of bookings holding a slot in space `spaceExpr` that overlap the
// window [startParam, endParam). Windows that only touch do not overlap.
export function overlappingBookingsSql(spaceExpr: string, startParam: string, endParam: string, holdParam: string): string {
  return `(SELECT count(*)::int FROM bookings b
    WHERE b.space_id = ${spaceExpr}
      AND b.start_time < ${endParam}::timestamptz
      AND b.end_time > ${startParam}::timestamptz
      AND ${holdsSlotSql(holdParam)})`;
}

// True when the window falls on one IST day inside the space's opening hours.
// `s` is the parking_spaces alias.
export function withinOpeningHoursSql(startParam: string, endParam: string): string {
  const start = `(${startParam}::timestamptz AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata')`;
  const end = `(${endParam}::timestamptz AT TIME ZONE 'Asia/Kolkata')`;
  return `(${start}::date = ${end}::date
    AND ${start}::time >= s.open_time
    AND ${end}::time <= s.close_time)`;
}

Bash: Read the booking service, payment verification, overstay rule and auth code
sed -n 1,200p src/booking-service.ts; echo -----; cat src/payments.ts src/overstay.ts src/middleware/auth.ts src/session.ts
// Creating, paying for and reading bookings. The same rules apply whether the
// booking belongs to a logged-in customer or to a guest without an account.
import { randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
import { holdsSlotSql, overlappingBookingsSql, withinOpeningHoursSql } from "./availability";
import { config } from "./config";
import { pool } from "./db";
import { createOrder, isValidRazorpaySignature } from "./payments";

const MIN_BOOKING_MS = 30 * 60 * 1000; // 30 minutes
const MAX_BOOKING_MS = 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; // 24 hours

export type Failure = { ok: false; status: number; error: string };
const fail = (status: number, error: string): Failure => ({ ok: false, status, error });

// Who a new booking is for.
export type BookingOwner =
  | { userId: number }
  | { guest: { name: string; phone: string; ip: string | null } };

// A booking with its space, plus whether an unpaid hold has run out.
// $1 is always the hold duration in minutes.
export const BOOKING_SELECT = `
  SELECT b.id, b.user_id, b.space_id, b.start_time, b.end_time, b.amount, b.status,
         b.qr_token, b.created_at, b.guest_name, b.guest_phone, b.access_token,
         s.name AS space_name, s.address AS space_address,
         (b.status = 'pending' AND NOT ${holdsSlotSql("$1")}) AS is_expired,
         b.created_at + make_interval(mins => $1::int) AS hold_expires_at,
         (SELECT p.provider_order_id FROM payments p
          WHERE p.booking_id = b.id ORDER BY p.id DESC LIMIT 1) AS order_id,
         v.entry_at, v.exit_at, v.overstay_minutes, v.overstay_amount, v.overstay_settlement
  FROM bookings b
  JOIN parking_spaces s ON s.id = b.space_id
  LEFT JOIN visits v ON v.booking_id = b.id`;

// JSON for a booking, as its customer or guest sees it. The QR link is only
// given out once the booking is paid. `forGuest` adds the guest's own details
// and the token their receipt link is built from.
export function toBooking(row: Record<string, unknown>, forGuest = false) {
  const {
    qr_token, user_id: _userId, order_id: _orderId, guest_name, guest_phone, access_token,
    entry_at, exit_at, overstay_minutes, overstay_amount, overstay_settlement,
    ...rest
  } = row;
  return {
    ...rest,
    // Once the vehicle has left: when it came and went, and any overstay charge.
    visit: exit_at
      ? { entry_at, exit_at, overstay_minutes, overstay_amount: Number(overstay_amount), overstay_settlement }
      : null,
    amount: Number(row.amount),
    reference: `PSZ-${String(row.id).padStart(6, "0")}`,
    verify_url: row.status === "confirmed" ? `${config.appBaseUrl}/verify/${qr_token}` : null,
    ...(forGuest ? { guest_name, guest_phone, access_token } : {}),
  };
}

// One booking matching `where` (written against BOOKING_SELECT; its own
// parameters start at $2), or null.
export async function findBooking(where: string, params: unknown[]) {
  const { rows } = await pool.query(`${BOOKING_SELECT} WHERE ${where}`, [config.bookingHoldMinutes, ...params]);
  return rows[0] ?? null;
}

function parseDate(value: unknown): Date | null {
  if (typeof value !== "string") return null;
  const date = new Date(value);
  return Number.isNaN(date.getTime()) ? null : date;
}

// Holds a slot and starts a payment. The amount is always worked out here from
// the space's price. In one transaction the space row is locked, so two people
// cannot both take the last slot.
export async function createBooking(
  body: Record<string, unknown> | undefined,
  owner: BookingOwner
): Promise<{ ok: true; bookingId: number; orderId: string } | Failure> {
  const rawSpaceId = body?.space_id;
  const spaceId = typeof rawSpaceId === "number" && Number.isInteger(rawSpaceId) && rawSpaceId > 0 ? rawSpaceId : null;
  const start = parseDate(body?.start);
  const end = parseDate(body?.end);
  if (!spaceId) return fail(400, "space_id is required");
  if (!start || !end) return fail(400, "start and end must be valid date-times");
  if (end <= start) return fail(400, "End time must be after start time");
  if (start.getTime() <= Date.now()) return fail(400, "Start time must be in the future");
  const durationMs = end.getTime() - start.getTime();
  if (durationMs < MIN_BOOKING_MS || durationMs > MAX_BOOKING_MS) {
    return fail(400, "A booking must be between 30 minutes and 24 hours long");
  }

  const guest = "guest" in owner ? owner.guest : null;
  let bookingId: number;
  let amount: number;
  const client = await pool.connect();
  try {
    await client.query("BEGIN");
    const space = (
      await client.query(
        `SELECT s.id, s.is_active, s.total_slots, s.price_per_hour,
                ${withinOpeningHoursSql("$2", "$3")} AS is_open
         FROM parking_spaces s WHERE s.id = $1 FOR UPDATE`,
        [spaceId, start.toISOString(), end.toISOString()]
      )
    ).rows[0];

    let problem: Failure | null = null;
    if (!space || !space.is_active) problem = fail(404, "Parking space not found");
    else if (!space.is_open) problem = fail(400, "The space is closed for some or all of that time");
    else {
      const { rows } = await client.query(
        `SELECT ${overlappingBookingsSql("$1", "$2", "$3", "$4")} AS taken`,
        [spaceId, start.toISOString(), end.toISOString(), config.bookingHoldMinutes]
      );
      if (rows[0].taken >= space.total_slots) problem = fail(409, "No slots are free for that time");
    }
    if (problem) {
      await client.query("ROLLBACK");
      return problem;
    }

    amount = Math.round(Number(space.price_per_hour) * (durationMs / 3_600_000) * 100) / 100;
    const inserted = await client.query(
      `INSERT INTO bookings
         (user_id, space_id, start_time, end_time, amount, status, qr_token,
          guest_name, guest_phone, guest_ip, access_token)
       VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, 'pending', $6, $7, $8, $9, $10) RETURNING id`,
      [
        "userId" in owner ? owner.userId : null,
        spaceId, start.toISOString(), end.toISOString(), amount,
        randomBytes(24).toString("base64url"),
        guest?.name ?? null, guest?.phone ?? null, guest?.ip ?? null,
        guest ? randomBytes(32).toString("base64url") : null,
      ]
    );
    bookingId = inserted.rows[0].id;
    await client.query("COMMIT");
  } catch (err) {
    await client.query("ROLLBACK");
    throw err;
  } finally {
    client.release();
  }

  // The slot is now held. Create the payment order outside the lock; if the
  // provider is unreachable, release the slot again.
  let orderId: string;
  try {
    orderId = await createOrder(`booking_${bookingId}`, amount);
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(err instanceof Error ? err.message : "Payment order failed");
    await pool.query("UPDATE bookings SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE id = $1", [bookingId]);
    return fail(502, "Could not start the payment. Please try again.");
  }
  await pool.query(
    "INSERT INTO payments (booking_id, provider_order_id, amount, status) VALUES ($1, $2, $3, 'created')",
    [bookingId, orderId, amount]
  );
  return { ok: true, bookingId, orderId };
}

// Checks the payment for `booking` (a BOOKING_SELECT row the caller is allowed
// to pay) and confirms it.
//   mock mode:     { mock_payment_id }
//   razorpay mode: { razorpay_order_id, razorpay_payment_id, razorpay_signature }
// Confirming an already confirmed booking changes nothing.
export async function confirmBooking(
  booking: Record<string, any>,
  body: Record<string, unknown> | undefined
): Promise<{ ok: true } | Failure> {
  if (booking.status === "confirmed") return { ok: true };
  if (booking.status === "cancelled") return fail(409, "This booking was cancelled");
  if (booking.is_expired) return fail(409, "This booking was not paid in time and has expired. Please book again.");

  let paymentId: string;
  if (config.paymentMode === "mock") {
    const mockId = body?.mock_payment_id;
    if (typeof mockId !== "string" || !/^mock_pay_[A-Za-z0-9]{1,40}$/.test(mockId)) {
      return fail(400, "mock_payment_id is required");
    }
    paymentId = mockId;
  } else {
    const { razorpay_order_id, razorpay_payment_id, razorpay_signature } = body ?? {};
    if (
      typeof razorpay_order_id !== "string" ||
      typeof razorpay_payment_id !== "string" ||
      typeof razorpay_signature !== "string"
    ) {
      return fail(400, "Payment details are missing");
    }
    if (
      razorpay_order_id !== booking.order_id ||
      !isValidRazorpaySignature(razorpay_order_id, razorpay_payment_id, razorpay_signature)
    ) {
      return fail(400, "Payment could not be verified");
    }
    paymentId = razorpay_payment_id;
  }

  // Confirm only if still pending and still inside the hold, so a booking
  // cannot be confirmed twice or after its slot was released.
  const client = await pool.connect();
-----
// Payment provider calls, used for bookings and for rental invoices.
// Two modes share one flow:
//   mock     — no provider; the in-app test screen reports pay or fail.
//   razorpay — a Razorpay order is created here and Checkout runs in the browser.
import { createHmac, randomBytes, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
import { config } from "./config";

// Creates the provider order for something to be paid and returns its id.
// `receipt` is our own label for it, e.g. "booking_12" or "rental_7".
export async function createOrder(receipt: string, amountRupees: number): Promise<string> {
  if (config.paymentMode === "mock") return `mock_order_${randomBytes(9).toString("hex")}`;

  const auth = Buffer.from(`${config.razorpayKeyId}:${config.razorpayKeySecret}`).toString("base64");
  const res = await fetch("https://api.razorpay.com/v1/orders", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { Authorization: `Basic ${auth}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      amount: Math.round(amountRupees * 100), // Razorpay amounts are in paise
      currency: "INR",
      receipt,
    }),
  });
  if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`Razorpay order creation failed with status ${res.status}`);
  const order = (await res.json()) as { id?: unknown };
  if (typeof order.id !== "string") throw new Error("Razorpay order response had no id");
  return order.id;
}

// Razorpay signs "<order_id>|<payment_id>" with the key secret (HMAC-SHA256, hex).
export function isValidRazorpaySignature(orderId: string, paymentId: string, signature: string): boolean {
  const expected = createHmac("sha256", config.razorpayKeySecret).update(`${orderId}|${paymentId}`).digest("hex");
  const given = Buffer.from(signature, "utf8");
  const wanted = Buffer.from(expected, "utf8");
  return given.length === wanted.length && timingSafeEqual(given, wanted);
}

// What the browser needs to start paying for an order.
export function paymentInfo(orderId: string | null) {
  return {
    mode: config.paymentMode,
    order_id: orderId,
    currency: "INR",
    ...(config.paymentMode === "razorpay" ? { key_id: config.razorpayKeyId } : {}),
  };
}
// The overstay rule. A vehicle that leaves after its booked end time may owe
// an extra charge, which the attendant collects at the gate.
//
//   late minutes = whole minutes between the booked end and now (server clock)
//   up to OVERSTAY_GRACE_MINUTES late         -> free
//   later than that                           -> billed from the booked end, in
//       started blocks of OVERSTAY_BLOCK_MINUTES, each block costing
//       price_per_hour x (block / 60) x OVERSTAY_RATE_PERCENT %
//
// With the defaults (10 min grace, 30 min blocks, 100 %) at 40 per hour:
//   10 min late -> 0, 11 min -> 20, 30 min -> 20, 31 min -> 40, 50 min -> 40.
import { config } from "./config";

export const SETTLEMENTS = ["cash", "upi", "waived"] as const;
export type Settlement = (typeof SETTLEMENTS)[number];

// Price of one overstay block at a space.
export function blockAmount(pricePerHour: number): number {
  const amount = pricePerHour * (config.overstayBlockMinutes / 60) * (config.overstayRatePercent / 100);
  return Math.round(amount * 100) / 100;
}

export function overstayAmount(lateMinutes: number, pricePerHour: number): number {
  if (lateMinutes <= config.overstayGraceMinutes) return 0;
  const blocks = Math.ceil(lateMinutes / config.overstayBlockMinutes);
  return Math.round(blocks * blockAmount(pricePerHour) * 100) / 100;
}

// The rule as numbers, sent with each visit so screens can explain the charge
// and show a live estimate. The hourly price is public information.
export function overstayRule(pricePerHour: number) {
  return {
    grace_minutes: config.overstayGraceMinutes,
    block_minutes: config.overstayBlockMinutes,
    block_amount: blockAmount(pricePerHour),
    price_per_hour: pricePerHour,
  };
}

// SQL: whole minutes from the end of booking `b` until now, never negative.
export const LATE_MINUTES_SQL = "GREATEST(floor(extract(epoch FROM now() - b.end_time) / 60), 0)::int";
import type { NextFunction, Request, Response } from "express";
import { SESSION_COOKIE, verifySession, type Role, type SessionUser } from "../session";

declare global {
  namespace Express {
    interface Request {
      user?: SessionUser;
    }
  }
}

// 401 unless the request carries a valid session cookie; sets req.user.
export function requireAuth(req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) {
  const user = verifySession(req.cookies?.[SESSION_COOKIE]);
  if (!user) return res.status(401).json({ error: "Please log in" });
  req.user = user;
  next();
}

// For public routes: anonymous visitors are let through (req.user stays
// undefined); a logged-in user must have one of the listed roles.
export function publicOr(...roles: Role[]) {
  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    const user = verifySession(req.cookies?.[SESSION_COOKIE]);
    if (user && !roles.includes(user.role)) {
      return res.status(403).json({ error: "You do not have access to this" });
    }
    if (user) req.user = user;
    next();
  };
}

// 401 if not logged in, 403 if logged in with a role that is not listed.
export function requireRole(...roles: Role[]) {
  return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
    requireAuth(req, res, () => {
      if (!req.user || !roles.includes(req.user.role)) {
        const message = roles.length === 1 ? `Only ${roles[0]}s can do this` : "You do not have access to this";
        return res.status(403).json({ error: message });
      }
      next();
    });
  };
}
import type { CookieOptions } from "express";
import jwt from "jsonwebtoken";
import { config } from "./config";

export type Role = "customer" | "owner" | "attendant" | "admin";

export interface SessionUser {
  id: number;
  username: string;
  role: Role;
}

export const SESSION_COOKIE = "parkspotz_session";
const SESSION_DAYS = 7;

export const sessionCookieOptions: CookieOptions = {
  httpOnly: true,
  sameSite: "lax",
  secure: config.isProduction,
  path: "/",
  maxAge: SESSION_DAYS * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000,
};

export function signSession(user: SessionUser): string {
  return jwt.sign({ username: user.username, role: user.role }, config.jwtSecret, {
    subject: String(user.id),
    expiresIn: `${SESSION_DAYS}d`,
    algorithm: "HS256",
  });
}

// Returns the user stored in a session token, or null if it is missing, expired or forged.
export function verifySession(token: unknown): SessionUser | null {
  if (typeof token !== "string" || !token) return null;
  try {
    const payload = jwt.verify(token, config.jwtSecret, { algorithms: ["HS256"] });
    if (typeof payload === "string") return null;
    const id = Number(payload.sub);
    const { username, role } = payload;
    if (!Number.isInteger(id) || typeof username !== "string") return null;
    if (!["customer", "owner", "attendant", "admin"].includes(role)) return null;
    return { id, username, role };
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

// The payment failed or was given up: record it and release the slot by
// cancelling the pending booking.
export async function failBooking(booking: Record<string, any>): Promise<{ ok: true } | Failure> {
  if (booking.status === "confirmed") return fail(409, "This booking is already paid");
  if (booking.status !== "pending") return { ok: true };

  const client = await pool.connect();
  try {
    await client.query("BEGIN");
    const updated = await client.query(
      "UPDATE bookings SET status = 'cancelled' WHERE id = $1 AND status = 'pending'",
      [booking.id]
    );
    if (updated.rowCount === 1) {
      await client.query("UPDATE payments SET status = 'failed' WHERE booking_id = $1 AND status = 'created'", [
        booking.id,
      ]);
    }
    await client.query("COMMIT");
  } catch (err) {
    await client.query("ROLLBACK");
    throw err;
  } finally {
    client.release();
  }
  return { ok: true };
}
